| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Palatine Lion |
| description | Originating from the dynastic coat of arms of the Wittelsbach family, the Palatine Lion is the centerpiece of our legitimacy. ### Palazzo Te

| name | man\_palazzo\_te |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Palazzo Te |
| description | Federico II Gonzaga commissioned Giulio Romano to build a pleasure palace on an island at the edge of the city, and what emerged was the *Palazzo Te*, one of the most inventive buildings of the Renaissance — a work of architecture that quotes the classical tradition with such knowing wit that scholars have argued over its ironies ever since. The frescoed rooms, from the giants collapsing in painted ruin to the horses of Federico's stables rendered with life-size confidence, are the most daring decorative programme in northern Italy. This extraordinary achievement deepens our cultural tradition and gives the works produced under our patronage a singular character that collectors and courts seek out above the more conventional output of our rivals. |
| icon | Advance patron of art.png |
| age | Reformation |

### Palladian Architecture

| name | ven\_mixed\_naval\_tactics |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Palladian Architecture |
| description | Andrea Palladio drew on the proportions of ancient Rome and the building materials of the Veneto to create a style that is simultaneously learned and practical, at home in a villa on the Brenta as in a civic loggia on a market square. His principles spread from Vicenza across every *Terraferma* city, giving our public buildings a dignity grounded in mathematics rather than fashion. This *Oordine Palladiano* not only beautifies our cities but makes them legible — a visitor entering our towns reads confidence, prosperity, and permanence in every facade. |
| icon | Advance city building advance.png |
| age | Reformation |
| ai\_preference\_tags |  |
| ai\_weight |  |

| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Palm Tree Masters |

### Pandurs

| name | cro\_pandurs\_recruitment |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Pandurs |
| description | The state must harness the ferocity of the frontier without succumbing to its chaos. The Pandurs — once mere night watchmen and local security — are reorganized by charismatic leaders into a professionalized light infantry. These soldiers are a terrifying sight on the European battlefield, accompanied by their own bands of martial music. They are the masters of skirmishes, specializing in raiding supply lines, screening the main army, and psychological warfare. While their discipline remains erratic and their propensity for plunder high, they provide a ruler with a flexible tool of intimidation that conventional line infantry cannot match. |

### Perpetual General Captain of the People

| name | perpetual\_general\_captain\_of\_the\_people |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Perpetual General Captain of the People |
| description | The Scaligeri have claimed the title of *Capitano del Popolo* not as a temporary office but as a permanent grant rooted in commune law, framing every military campaign as the defense of civic rights rather than dynastic ambition and making it far harder for rivals to cast our wars as mere aggression. Cangrande della Scala built his reputation on exactly this legitimacy — riding out against Milan, Venice, and Padua while presenting each conflict as a just cause — and we formalize his example by keeping the city's councils engaged in decisions that other lords make alone. This combination of legal standing and public mandate makes rivals reluctant to antagonize us openly, since any attack on Verona reads as an attack on the commune itself. |
| icon | Advance perpetual general captain of the people.png |

### Piacentine Administrative Reform

| name | pcz\_administrative\_reform |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Piacentine Administrative Reform |
| description | The rulers of Piacenza have governed a state cobbled together by nepotism and held together by the ruler family's unusual combination of military ruthlessness and administrative intelligence, and it is the latter quality that has proved more durable. We commit to a systematic reform of our fiscal and administrative institutions, bringing the practices of the most efficient Italian states to bear on a territory that has been run too long on personal authority rather than institutional procedure. This *Riforma Amministrativa* rewards our most capable subjects with advancement based on competence rather than connection and gives our burgher class a stake in the efficiency of a state whose tax collection finally rewards those who produce rather than those who extract. |
| icon | Advance smooth administration.png |

### Piacentine Soldiery

| name | pcz\_piacentine\_soldiery |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Piacentine Soldiery |
| description | The countryside of Piacenza has long produced soldiers of a quality that exceeds what the city's modest size would predict. Men raised on Po valley farmsteads and in the Apennine foothills endure a campaign without growing restless and hold a line without breaking, and we invest in the training, wages, and officers that turn these qualities into professional discipline. ### Pisan Marble Tradition

| name | pis\_marble\_tradition |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Pisan Marble Tradition |
| description | The quarries supplying Pisa and the surrounding coast have long produced some of the finest white marble in Italy, used in the great churches and civic monuments that define our skyline. Sculptors and architects trained in this tradition have become sought-after across the peninsula, and the stone itself commands high prices from patrons who wish to build in the Pisan manner. This mastery of stone raises our cultural standing and ensures that every contract signed and shipment sold carries with it the prestige of a city that knows how to make the world beautiful. |

### Pisan Medical Tradition

| name | pis\_medical\_tradition |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Pisan Medical Tradition |
| description | The medical faculty of the University of Pisa is anchored by the *Orto Botanico* — among the oldest university botanical gardens in the world — where students study the healing properties of plants and develop methods to treat epidemic disease more effectively. Physicians trained in Pisa carry this empirical tradition across the country, improving outcomes for the sick and reducing the toll that plague and pestilence exact on our population. This commitment to medical learning strengthens the resilience of our people and spreads the literacy that comes naturally to those trained in the careful arts of diagnosis and record-keeping. |

### Pisan Natural Philosophy

| name | pis\_natural\_philosophy |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Pisan Natural Philosophy |
| description | The scholars of Pisa have pursued understanding through careful observation and measurement rather than received authority alone, building on a long tradition of inquiry in our *Studio Pisano* that spans mechanics, astronomy, and the natural arts. This empirical habit of mind has shaped not only our philosophers but our engineers and commanders, who approach problems with the same rigor that our scholars apply to the motion of the heavens. This culture of natural philosophy disciplines our military thinking and gives our forces a tactical precision that more tradition-bound armies cannot match. |

### Platoon Fire

| name | platoon\_fire |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Platoon Fire |
| description | Standard volley fire involved each rank delivering its volley in one go to maximize firepower. But we have developed a different system of firing by platoon, with the idea being that one part of the regiment would always be firing at the enemy while others reloaded and got ready for the next volley. This removed time between attacks that the enemy could use to recover or otherwise gain an advantage. |

### Plethon's Vision

| name | moe\_plethons\_vision\_advance |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Plethon's Vision |
| description | The arrival of the philosopher Georgios Gemistus Plethon at the court of Mystras has signaled a radical shift in the Morean identity, as he has proposed a return to the laws and social structures of ancient Sparta. Plethon advocates for a national army of native Greeks rather than unreliable mercenaries, and a social order based on Hellenic heritage rather than just Christian confession. This proto-nationalist awakening will provide the Despotate with a powerful ideological weapon, transforming the defense of the Morea into a crusade for the survival of the Greek nation itself, which will bolster the prestige of our ruling house even if we stick to Christianity. |

### Po Valley Agriculture

| name | man\_po\_agriculture |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Po Valley Agriculture |
| description | The lowland plain that surrounds Mantua on three sides is among the most fertile land in Italy, and we have made systematic investment in its management the foundation of our domestic economy — draining marshes, expanding irrigation channels, and regulating the reclamation of new land at the margins of the lakes that define our geography. The result is an agricultural capacity that grows reliably each generation, supplying our city with food surpluses that sustain a population larger than our politics alone would justify. This patient cultivation of the *Pianüra Padâna* (*Pianura Padana* in standard Italian: the Po Plain) ensures that our subjects eat well in difficult years and that our treasury collects the taxes of a prosperous countryside. |
| icon | Advance agriculture advance.png |

### Ponte delle Torri

| name | spl\_ponte\_delle\_torri |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Ponte delle Torri |
| description | The ancient *Ponte delle Torri* — part Roman aqueduct, part fortified bridge — strides across the Tessino gorge on ten great piers, giving us control of the main Apennine passage between Umbria and the Marche. Merchants who wish to cross the mountains must cross our bridge, and we have never been reluctant to collect what that privilege is worth in tolls, intelligence, and deference. This structure makes our territory a natural chokepoint, pulling trade inward and making any would-be aggressor calculate the cost of that crossing before they march. |

| name | porto\_franco |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Porto Franco |
| description | In declaring Livorno a *Porto Franco*, we open our harbor to merchants of every faith and nation — Armenians, Jews, Greeks, and Flemings all welcomed without the religious conditions that other Mediterranean ports impose. This freedom draws skilled traders and financiers whose settlement enriches our city and swells our trade routes with goods that more closed ports cannot handle. This cosmopolitan harbor transforms Livorno from a modest fishing village into one of the most dynamic commercial ports in the western Mediterranean, drawing population and capital to our shores. |

### Porto Franco

| name | gen\_free\_port |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Porto Franco |
| description | As the great Atlantic trade routes have drawn commerce away from the Mediterranean, we have answered by declaring our harbor a *Porto Franco* — a free port open to merchants of all nations, exempt from the duties and restrictions that weigh down our rivals. Ships that once bypassed Liguria now put in at our quays, and the city fills again with foreign factors, warehouses, and coin. This openness to all comers turns our harbor into one of the busiest entrepôts of the western Mediterranean, drawing trade and people in equal measure. |

### Printing Industry

| name | ven\_printing\_industry |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Printing Industry |
| description | When Aldo Manuzio established the *Aldine Press* in Venice in 1494, we became the printing capital of the world almost overnight — our merchants had the gold to finance it, our scholars the Greek and Latin to edit it, and our trade routes to carry the finished books to every court in Europe. The italic typeface, the affordable pocket octavo, and the authoritative editions of Plato, Aristotle, and Virgil that Aldo produced set a standard that no press on the continent has since matched. This mastery of the book trade gives Venice a commercial and intellectual reach that extends far beyond what our galleys can carry, making the ideas that flow from our presses as valuable as the spices in our warehouses. |
| icon | Advance ven printing industry.png |

### Proactive Diplomacy

| name | sme\_proactive\_diplomacy |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Proactive Diplomacy |
| description | While still a part of Georgia, Samtskhe operates with substantial independence. This privileged position requires constant diplomatic awareness and a policy of active diplomacy, ensuring that we always have a direct channel to those in power. We do all this while actively engaging in our internal politics, meddling with the succession and forging dynastic alliances as best benefits us. The survival of our relatively small state depends on upholding these diplomatic traditions. Let it never be said that we have done too little or acted without sufficient wisdom! |

| name | por\_pursuing\_waves |

| display\_name | Pāli Canon |
| description | Although there are many different canons in Buddhism, depending on the school, the Theravāda Pāli Canon is the oldest and most prestigious. Other schools have created their canons from a mix of texts with various sources, but the Pāli Canon has remained unchanged since the times of Ashoka 'the Great', and contains the words of the Buddha himself. It has also served as the foundation of many other schools. |
